- **waimao-site**: 创建或改造面向海外采购商的 B2B 外贸网站，使用 Astro + Tailwind，内容来源可选本地文件、RXCMS API 或 WordPress REST API（实验，仅标准文章），覆盖页面规划、B2B 视觉设计、询盘内容和完整的站内技术 SEO（元信息、canonical、sitemap、结构化数据、多语言 hreflang）。新建站默认 static，可按需求选择 server，并支持 Vercel / Cloudflare 部署配置与迁移。用于外贸建站和相关实现，不代替独立的营销运营或通用 CMS 管理。 ([codebdy/waimao-site](https://github.com/codebdy/waimao-site/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md) · ★13)
- **Wainwright Architect**: Use when designing a system or API, modeling data, choosing between architectures, stacks, or patterns, making scaling and trade-off decisions, reviewing a design, or answering "how should we structure this" before code is written. Auto-applies from context. Explicit trade-offs, named assumptions, a committed recommendation (INTJ-A / Magician profile). Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-architect) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Brainstorm**: Use when generating options before evaluating them: "let's brainstorm", "what are my options", "what am I missing", "stress-test this plan", or when the problem definition itself may be wrong. Auto-applies from context. High-volume divergent ideas and challenges to the framing (ENTP / Explorer profile). Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-brainstorm) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Coding**: Use when implementing code, reviewing code or a PR/diff, or refactoring, where a working, reviewed artifact is the deliverable. Auto-applies from context. Systematic, closure-seeking, blunt about code (INTJ / Ruler profile). For an active bug use Wainwright Debug; for system design use Wainwright Architect. Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-coding) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Core**: Use when an honest assessment is more useful than diplomatic softening, when earlier turns drifted into flattery, padding, excessive caveats, approval-seeking, or false balance, or when the user says "be direct", "don't sugarcoat it", "honest feedback", "stop hedging", "what's actually wrong", or "your real opinion". Auto-applies from context. The shared anti-sycophancy baseline every other Wainwright persona builds on. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-core) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Creative**: Use when producing or critiquing original creative work: narrative, fiction, poetry, copy, design, or art direction, especially when the user wants a genuine read rather than reassurance. Auto-applies from context. Imaginative reach with values-grounded critique (INFP / Creator profile). For docs or explanations use Wainwright Writing. Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-creative) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Curator**: Use when checking whether docs, notes, or a wiki are still accurate, auditing or reorganizing a knowledge base, reconciling contradictions, or pruning stale and duplicated content. Auto-applies from context. Order, maintenance, and a drift radar for things that quietly stopped being true (ISTJ-A / Archivist profile). For new explanatory prose use Wainwright Writing. Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-curator) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Data**: Use when interpreting data, statistics, A/B tests, metrics, dashboards, ML evaluations, or any "the numbers show" claim where a hidden confounder or motivated reading could mislead. Auto-applies from context. Skeptical and precise (ISTJ-A / Analyzer profile). Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-data) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Debug**: Use when chasing a bug, crash, stack trace, failing or flaky test, regression, "it works on my machine", or any "why is this happening" where the cause is unknown and guessing is tempting. Auto-applies from context. Repro-first, evidence-only, hypothesis-driven (ISTP-A troubleshooter profile). Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-debug) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Decide**: Use when prioritizing, scoping, triaging features, making a go/no-go call, or answering "should we build this" or "what should I cut", where the useful answer may be no, smaller, or later. Auto-applies from context. Unsentimental about trade-offs (ESTJ-A / Executive profile). Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-decide) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Logic**: Use when the user says "analyze this", "is this argument valid", "help me think through", or "is this actually correct", when stress-testing reasoning, or when spotting logical fallacies, where the correct conclusion matters more than a fast one. Auto-applies from context. Adversarial premise-testing, evidence-first (INTP / Sage profile). For a code bug use Wainwright Debug. Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-logic) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Onboard**: Use when this is a first wake, the user asks to install or set up Wainwright, or they ask to onboard a Grok Bot fleet. Auto-applies from context in those cases; do not wait for an @mention. Plain pitch, one pick menu, defaults and a progress checklist, then hire only the roles they pick. After each verified CreateAgent, SendToAgent Teammate Focus Onboard. Quiet when nothing to onboard. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-onboard) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Security**: Use when reviewing code, configs, or designs for security: auth, crypto, input validation, injection, secrets, permissions, SSRF, deserialization, or supply-chain risk, on systems you own or are authorized to test. Auto-applies from context. Adversarial, low-trust threat modeling, defensive scope only (ISTJ-T / Sentinel profile). Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-security) · ★2)
- **Wainwright Writing**: Use when writing or editing docs, explanations, READMEs, tutorials, guides, or summaries where the goal is to transfer understanding. Auto-applies from context. Leads with the point, cuts jargon and filler, flags the hard part (ENFJ-A / Mentor profile). Builds on Wainwright Core. ([itsryanlenk/wainwright](https://github.com/itsryanlenk/wainwright/tree/HEAD/skills/wainwright-writing) · ★2)

- **wait-what**: Recognize when the last explanation didn't land, and re-pitch it rather than repeating it. ([aethrox/doctrine](https://github.com/aethrox/doctrine/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★19)

- **wait-what**: Restates the last message in plain human language, with no jargon. Use for /wait-what or when the user asks to say it plainly. ([richardochristjia/skills](https://github.com/richardochristjia/skills/tree/HEAD/wait-what) · ★3)
- **wait-what**: Stop. That last message did not land — re-pitch it. Trigger: wait what, I don't understand, lost me, what do you mean, huh, confused. ([smileynet/teach-me](https://github.com/smileynet/teach-me/tree/HEAD/.kiro/skills/wait-what) · ★3)
- **wait-what**: Stop — that last message did not land. Re-pitch it from a running start, in plain controlled English, keeping the project's domain terms intact. Trigger ONLY on an explicit signal that the user did not follow the previous explanation - /wait-what, "wait, what?", "I don't follow", "you lost me", "say that again". Do NOT fire when "wait, what" signals alarm at an action rather than confusion at an explanation ("wait what, you deleted it?") - answer for the action instead. Adapted from Matt Pocock's wait-what skill (github.com/mattpocock/skills). ([AhmedMerza/agent-skills](https://github.com/AhmedMerza/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★2)
- **wait-what**: Stop. That last message did not land: explain it again, simpler. ([andres-ortizl/dotfiles](https://github.com/andres-ortizl/dotfiles/tree/HEAD/config/claude/skills/wait-what) · ★2)
- **wait-what**: Stop. The last message was not understood. Re-pitch it using simplified language. ([austek/ustekai-skills](https://github.com/austek/ustekai-skills/tree/HEAD/productivity/wait-what) · ★2)
- **wait-what**: Use when the user explicitly says the previous explanation did not land and asks for it to be re-pitched more clearly. ([BigPizzaV3/CodexPlusPlusPluginCache](https://github.com/BigPizzaV3/CodexPlusPlusPluginCache/tree/HEAD/plugins/mattpocock-skills/skills/wait-what) · ★2)
- **wait-what**: Re-pitch an explanation or proposal from a simpler angle with reset assumptions and plain English. ([BigPizzaV3/CodexPlusPlusPluginCache](https://github.com/BigPizzaV3/CodexPlusPlusPluginCache/tree/HEAD/plugins/matt-skills-curated/skills/wait-what) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **wait-what**: 停。刚才那条没说明白：重讲一遍。 ([chenyongquan2/plain-chinese-skills](https://github.com/chenyongquan2/plain-chinese-skills/tree/HEAD/wait-what) · ★2)

- **wait-what**: Re-pitch something that did not land. Use when a person pushes back in a way that suggests they read something different from what you meant, when the same question comes back twice, or when you are about to repeat yourself louder. Diagnoses WHY the message missed before rephrasing it. ([rajanrx/skills](https://github.com/rajanrx/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★2)
- **wait-what**: Stop — that last message didn't land. Re-pitch it in plain language with the context I'm missing. ([risadams/ink-and-agency](https://github.com/risadams/ink-and-agency/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★2)
- **wait-what**: User-invoked repair command for a message that did not land. Use when the user types /wait-what after failing to follow the assistant's last message. The assistant re-pitches what it just said, adds the missing context, writes in the conversation's controlled plain register (ASD-STE100 for English, controlled Chinese otherwise), and swaps invented shorthand for the canonical terms in the repo-root CONTEXT.md. ([SipengXie2024/superpower-writing](https://github.com/SipengXie2024/superpower-writing/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★2)

- **wait-what**: Re-pitches the agent's last message after it did not land, with context first, every label explained, and plain short sentences. - **wait-what**: Stop. That last message did not land: re-pitch it. Use when the user says they did not follow your last message. ([Zeldris/Galivelop-workflow](https://github.com/Zeldris/Galivelop-workflow/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/wait-what) · ★2 · 9 copies)

- **wait-what**: Stop. That last message did not land: re-pitch it. Do not use to correct an error — re-pitching is about comprehension, not correctness. ([georgernstgraf/opencode-helpers](https://github.com/georgernstgraf/opencode-helpers/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★1)

- **wait-what**: 重讲 agent 写的话：本人看不懂 agent 的回复、推送或报告，要换成大白话（「这条推送啥意思」「你刚才说的没看懂」「说人话」「wait what」）。本人看不懂的是题目、课件或概念时，直接讲内容。 ([incredible404-rgb/zstack](https://github.com/incredible404-rgb/zstack/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★1)

- **wait-what**: Use when the owner can't follow where the work has got to and wants it re-pitched - "wait, what?", "I don't understand where you've got to", "re-pitch that", "I'm lost", "say that in plain English" or "in Simplified Technical English" - or invokes /wait-what. ([manastalukdar/ai-devstudio](https://github.com/manastalukdar/ai-devstudio/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★1)

- **wait-what**: The last explanation did not land. Re-explain it with enough context to understand where we are and what happens next. ([mriekki/project-forge-platform](https://github.com/mriekki/project-forge-platform/tree/HEAD/assets/generated/core/core/skills/wait-what) · ★1)

- **wait-what**: Stop. That last message did not land — re-pitch it. Use when the user answers with confusion rather than a decision — «لم أفهم» · «ما قصدك» · «وضّح». ([Rashed9999/Anjaz](https://github.com/Rashed9999/Anjaz/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/wait-what) · ★1)

([Saifallah-star/AEGIS](https://github.com/Saifallah-star/AEGIS/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/wait-what) · ★1)
- **wait-what**: Recadre et réexplique la dernière intervention lorsque l'explication précédente n'a pas été comprise, en simplifiant le langage et en utilisant le glossaire CONTEXT.md. ([Skyl9/AnkiDraft](https://github.com/Skyl9/AnkiDraft/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/wait-what) · ★1)
- **wait-what**: The user types this when a message didn't land — re-pitch what you just said, shorter AND with the context they were missing, in plain English, using the vocabulary from the project's CONTEXT.md. "Wait" names the listener's state, not the output, so the fix is both fewer words and the missing premise, not a terse rewrite. Use when the user says "wait what", "you lost me", "explain that again", "in English", or is clearly skimming. ([tejastn10/workbench](https://github.com/tejastn10/workbench/tree/HEAD/skills/quality/wait-what) · ★1)

- **wait-what**: Re-pitch a message in ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English with canonical glossary terms and item names in place of artifact codes. Use before any message a human reads that refers to grill, spec, architecture, plan, or review items; when a message, term, or proposed wording does not land; when presenting grilling questions and recommendations; or when checking a complete plan before writing it. ([wearedevpunks/skills](https://github.com/wearedevpunks/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/misc/wait-what) · ★1)
- **wait-what**: Use when the operator signals the last message did not land - "wait", "what?", "you lost me", "what does that mean?" - or invokes it by name after a reply they could not act on. ([yoelgal/better-dev](https://github.com/yoelgal/better-dev/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★1)
- **wait-what**: Re-express a previous answer or supplied text clearly, preserving its meaning and language. ([zznabil/lean-agent-skill-collection](https://github.com/zznabil/lean-agent-skill-collection/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what) · ★1)
- **wait-what**: Wait, I don't understand where you've got to here. Re-pitch that: give me a little bit of context, talk in ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English, and use the ubiquitous language from `GLOSSARY.md` (follow `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` to the right one if the repo has more than one). ([1CECub3zz/mattpocock-gemini-skills-download](https://github.com/1CECub3zz/mattpocock-gemini-skills-download/tree/HEAD/skills/productivity/wait-what))
- **wait-what**: Re-pitch a message that did not land, in plain language grounded in the repo's vocabulary. Use when the user says they are lost or confused, asks what you just said, or pushes back on too much text or jargon. ([aakshintala/switchyard](https://github.com/aakshintala/switchyard/tree/HEAD/skills/wait-what))
- **wait-what**: Re-pitches the agent's last message when the user did not follow it, with a line of context first, then ASD-STE100 Simplified Technical English in the words of the project's GLOSSARY.md. Use whenever the user shows confusion about what the agent just said, in any wording, such as "what are you talking about", "I don't get it", "that makes no sense", "wait, what?", "huh?", "I'm lost", "you lost me", "what do you mean?", "I don't follow", "too much jargon", "say that in plain English" or a bare "???". ([akmolsinc/skills](https://github.com/akmolsinc/skills/tree/HEAD/skills/productivity/wait-what) · 2 copies)
- **wait-what**: Explicit-only. Use when the user says they did not understand your last message ("wait, what?", "I don't follow", "explain that again", "attends, je n'ai pas compris"). Re-explain that message in plain technical language, in the user's own language, without shortening it. Not for new questions or new work. - **wait-what**: Corrective for a message that did not land. When the user says "wait what", "I don't understand", "that makes no sense", "re-pitch", or otherwise signals confusion mid-conversation, stop and re-pitch the last point with the missing context, in plain language, using the CONTEXT.md vocabulary. Use when a message missed, the user seems lost, or the user asks to re-pitch, rephrase, or explain again. ([arendon1/agent-skills](https://github.com/arendon1/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/process/wait-what))

- **wake-provider**: The wake contract a cnos package declares to provide a wake.
